   ADMINISTRATIONBUILDING     
 PresidentPhilippartcalledthemeetingtoorderat7:00p.m.

InadditiontoMs.Philippart,presentwereBoardmembers
Edmunds,Fleming,Hauff,Miletti,SchmidtandUnderwood.Also
presentwereSuperintendentFowler;administratorsColligan,Rajter,
andWitt;andapproximately4membersoftheaudience.



None.

PUBLICCOMMUNICATION

BonnieOmsteadandherdaughter,LisaOmstead,addressedthe
BoardregardingthenumberofslotsavailablefortheDevelopmental
KindergartenprogramofferedintheDistrictandaskedtheBoardto
considerexpandingtheprograminordertoaccommodateall
childrenonthewaitlistwhoareinterestedinattending.


ItwasmovedbyMr.MilettiandsupportedbyMrs.Flemingthatbe
itthereforeresolvedthatthesix(6)secondaryschoolswhichare
underthedirectionofthisBoardofEducation/GoverningBodyof
theTroySchoolDistrict,City
ofTroy,CountyofOakland,ofthestate
ofMichigan,arehereby:





(A) enrolledasmembersoftheMichiganHighSchoolAthletic
Association,Inc.,anonprofitassociation,and
(B) arefurtherenrolledtoparticipateintheapproved
interschoolathleticactivitiessponsoredbysaidassociation.

TheBoardofEducation/GoverningBodyherebydelegatestothe
Superintendentorhis/herdesignee(s)theresponsibilityforthe
supervisionandcontrolofsaidactivitiesandherebyacceptsthe
ConstitutionandByͲLawsofsaidassociationandadoptsasitsown
therules,regulationsandinterpretations(asminimumstandards)as
publishedinthecurrentHANDBOOKasthegoverningcodeunder
whichthesaidschool(s)shallconductitsprogramofinterscholastic
athleticsandagreestoprimaryenforcementofsaidrules,
regulations,interpretationsandqualifications.Inaddition,itis
herebyagreedthatschoolswhichhostorparticipateinthe
association’smeetsandtournamentsshallfollowandenforceall
tournamentpolicies,proceduresandschedules.

ThisauthorizationshallbeeffectivefromAugust1,2012,andshall
remaineffectiveuntilJuly31,2013,duringwhichtheauthorization
maynotberevoked.


ItwasmovedbyMr.Schmidtand
supportedbyMs.Underwoodthat
beitthereforeresolvedthatthebidforSmartBoardsbeawardedto
AVIͲSPLintheamountof$180,344.00andthat$178,830.00ofthis
amountbeapprovedasanexpenditureofthe2004CapitalProjects
Fund,and$1,514.00ofthisamountbeapprovedasanexpenditure
ofthe2012Ͳ2013GeneralFund.

Yes: 7
No: 0

Mr.Schmidtinquiredastothehigherpriceforthispurchaseof
additionalsmartboardsthaninthepast.Ms.Colliganrepliedthat
therewasapriceincreaseinMayof2012.Healsoaskedaboutthe
location
ofanyextrasmartboards.Ms.Colliganrespondedthat
theyhavenotbeenassignedyetbecauseclassroomsarestillbeing
movedandthespecialeddepartmentisstilldeterminingwhetheror
notallclassroomswillbeabletoutilizethesmartboards.

Mr.Hauffaskedifthesenewsmartboardsarethenextgeneration
oftechnologyorthesameasourcurrentones.Ms.Colliganstated
thattheyarestilltheoneͲtouchsmartboards.

Mr.Milettiaskedwhetherornottheteachersknowtheyare
receivingasmartboardoriftheywillbemadeaware.Ms.Colligan
confirmedthattheywillbenotifiedandwillreceivetrainingduring
theAugustprofessionaldevelopmentdays.

Trademarks,ServiceMarksandTradeNames.
ItwasmovedbyMr.MilettiandsupportedbyMs.Underwoodthat
beitthereforeresolvedthattheTroyBoardofEducationapprove
andadoptBoardpoliciesJͲ2013andKͲ1601.

Yes: 7
No: 0

Dr.Fowlerinformedthe
Boardthatfinalinterviewsfortheposition
ofAssistantPrincipalatBakerMiddleSchoolisscheduledoverthe
nexttwodaysandthattheDistrictispreparedtomakea
recommendationtotheBoardfollowingthoseinterviews.She
indicatedthataspecialBoardofEducationmeetingwouldneedto
bescheduledforthefollowingweektoapprovetheappointment.
TheBoarddiscusseddatesandtimesforaspecialmeetingand
decidedthataspecialmeetingtoconsidertheappointmentof
AssistantPrincipal,BakerMiddleSchool,wouldbeheldnext
Tuesday,July17that5:30p.m.intheAdministrationBuilding.
SeveralBoardmembersstatedthattheywouldbeunabletoattend
butfourmembersconfirmedtheirparticipation,whichwouldmeet
thecriteriaforaquorum.

Ms.Philippartsharedthatpriortothisevening’sBoardmeeting,the
Superintendent’sEvaluationCommitteemembersmettodiscuss
the
newevaluationprocessandstatedthattheywouldbemeetingagain
inAugust.SheaskedthatBoardmemberssendheranysuggestions
orspecificsongoalsfortheupcomingschoolyear.

Mrs.EdmundsinquiredabouttheDistrictworkingundertheState
guidelinestomeettheintentofthelaw.Ms.Philippartconfirmed
thattheStatehascertainrequirementsintheirevaluationprocess
forteachersandadministratorsbutthereissomeflexibilityandthat
theDistrictmaychoosetouseahybridprocessconsistentwiththe
evaluationprocesssincethesuperintendentprocesshasunique
responsibilities.Ms.Philippartinformedtheaudiencethatthenext
regularBoardmeetingwouldbeheldonAugust14th.


It was moved by Mrs. Fleming and supported by Mr. Hauff 
that the meeting be adjourned.

 7. B.   08.14.12 
7. CURRICULUM 
B. REPORT:  NCLB Waiver Implications  
Mrs. Anne Mull, Director of Instruction and Assessment, will 
provide the Board with an overvie w of the implications for the 
State of Michigan’s waiver of the No Child Left Behind  (NCLB) 
requirements.  
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  This evening’s report will be presented by Mrs. 
Anne Mull.  No action is required.   
151
NCLB Waiver Implications 
August 14, 2012 
152
NCLB  
 
•  Under the Elementary and Secondary 
Education Act (ESEA) NCLB Adequate Yearly 
Progress (AYP) requirement, all Michigan 
schools were required to have 100% of their 
students proficient in mathematics and 
reading by 2014.  
• The U.S. Department of Education (USED) 
offered States the opportunity to request 
flexibility from the requirements of NCLB in 
exchange for rigorous and comprehensive 
plans designed to improve educational 
outcomes for all students, close 
achievement gaps, increase equity, and 
improve the quality of instruction.  
• Michigan received approval July 19, 2012

School Proficiency Targets (AMOs)

School C
More about the new AMOs--Michigan now has new Annual Measurable 
Objectives (AMOs) using the 2011-2012 school year as the baseline and 
allowing schools another 10 years to achieve 85% proficiency. 
 
156
Subgroups 
• Retain original nine demographic subgroups 
– Concern was voiced with dropping these, loss of 
focus on these groups 
– 6 Ethnicities 
– Students with IEPs 
– English Language Learners 
– Economically Disadvantaged 
• Add the bottom 30% subgroup 
 
157
More about the new identifications for 
schools…  
• The new identification method for accountability 
includes:  
– a. Priority Schools: Schools that are in the lowest 
performing group of schools in the state.  
– b. Focus Schools: Schools that have the largest gaps 
in achievement between their highest and lowest 
performing subgroups of students.  
– c. Continuous Improvement Schools: Schools that are 
not Priority, Focus, or Reward Schools.  
– d. Reward Schools: Schools that have consistently 
high academic achievement, large growth in 
achievement or have demonstrated that they are 
beating the odds.  
 
158
Our schools… 
 
Priority Schools: None in the Troy School 
District 
Focus Schools:  Athens, Baker, Boulan, 
Larson, Smith, Troy Union, Morse, 
Hamilton, Leonard, Bemis, Barnard, 
Wattles 
Continuous Improvement Schools: Martell, 
Troy High School 
Reward Schools: Costello, Hill, Schroeder, 
Wass 
 159
In Depth with Reward Schools 
Schools in the top 5% of the Top-to-Bottom 
ranking. 
Schools designated as a “Beating the Odds” 
school (schools that are outperforming 
schools with similar risk factors and 
demographic makeup) 
Schools in the top 5% of schools on the 
improvement metric in the Top-to-Bottom 
ranking 
160
In Depth with Focus Schools 
 
 What are focus schools? 
• Schools with the largest achievement gaps, defined as the difference 
between the average scale score for the top 30% of students and 
the bottom 30% of students. 
• Derived from the achievement gap component within the Top-to-
Bottom ranking.  
How are they determined?: 
• Based on the Top-to-Bottom (TTB) ranking methodology which 
includes data from achievement, improvement and achievement 
gap standardized scores. 
• All open schools with 2 years of data for 30 or more students two or 
more tested subjects are included in the ranking to determine focus 
schools. 
161
More on Focus Schools 
 
 What happens once a school is named a Focus 
School?  
• Michigan Department of Education will offer 
support in:  
• analyzing achievement data  
• facilitating professional dialogue  
• customizing interventions 
• MDE will develop and provide a District Toolkit  
• Designed for districts that have schools 
identified as Focus Schools  
• It is meant to help districts identify where their 
schools need the most support. 
 
162
More on Focus Schools 
• MDE will provide technical assistance to districts on 
the use of this toolkit in the form of MDE-trained 
and paid-for District Improvement Facilitators 
(DIFs).  
• With the assistance of their DIFs, these districts will 
have one year to self-diagnose and self-prescribe 
changes in their supports to Focus Schools based 
on the resources in the toolkit.  
• If a district continues to have schools identified as 
Focus Schools in the 2013/14 school year, the 
district will purchase the services of the District 
Improvement Facilitator to conduct a data-based 
professional dialogue to determine the district-
level needs to be put in place to support its 
school(s). 
163
Simplified reporting beginning next year… 
• Whole school and each subgroup receive green, 
red or yellow for each subject. 
– Red = did not meet proficiency OR improvement 
– Yellow = met improvement target, not proficiency 
– Green = met proficiency target 
• Must assess 95% of students overall and in each 
subgroup 
• Failure to do so in a subgroup = red for that 
subgroup/subject 
• Two “reds” in the all student category for 
participation = red overall

179
Region 8 — three year term
Robert Kirby Wyandotte Public Schools Wayne County
Time served on this board: 11 years
Offices held: President, Vice President and Treasurer
MASB Certification: 
 Certified Boardmember (CBA)
 Award of Merit 
 Award of Distinction 
 Master Boardmember
Election Statement:
In the ever changing dynamics of public education, good governance is a must, not only for every school district in 
the state of Michigan, but for every single school board member. I believe that we as policy makers must educate 
ourselves not only on the issues we face in our districts but those issues that affect us at the state and federal levels 
of government. The key here is education. I think it should be a must for board members to take classes to educate 
themselves on how to understand what issues they will face as board members. A new board member will be faced 
with so much information and for some it can take years to understand what the information means, but having 
a great knowledge base comes from the willingness to network and educate oneself (CBA courses are a positive 
step in doing this). Funding from the state will always be a topic of concern for every school district in the state of 
Michigan, but understanding the different types of funding and how your school district receives all of its revenue is 
something each board member should take very seriously. 
I have seen many new school board members think they can learn on the fly, but the fact is with the ever changing 
legislation/laws they make decisions from their heart and not their head. Some school boards don’t value profes-
sional development for their fellow board member as much as they should. The fact is the public has placed a 
large responsibility on the people they elect to be school board trustees. School board trustees need to take on that 
responsibility head on and educate themselves to keep the public’s trust.
My goal would be to work with every school district to get the information and professional development that is 
needed when and where it is needed. I would like to work with each and every County Association School Board to 
bring classes and/or seminars that are up-to-date and real time relevant so that the knowledge that is sought is not 
only available but consistent state wide. Helping school board trustees be better board members should be para-
mount.  
To be a true ambassador of education one must first educate themselves.

Election Statement:
Hello,
I am asking for your support for the MASB Board of Directors. There are many challenges facing school board 
members in Michigan. MASB has stood up and addressed these challenges and provided the needed tools to help 
local school districts.
I have served on my local board for 16 years. My board experiences include work on several MASB committees. I 
have severed on the Resolutions and Bylaws, Government Relations and Board Leadership and Training commit-
tees. I have also served on the MASB Michigan Council of Urban Boards of Education. On my local board, I have 
served as Board President, Vice President and Treasurer. I have chaired our Policy and Finance committees and 
served on our School Improvement committee. I am also a member of our Westwood Education Foundation Board.
I believe that we have dedicated school board associations that brings current and up-to-date educational materials, 
services and information to its membership. I will be proud to continue to support our local boards of education.

Region 8 — three year term ( continued)
Earl C. Rickman Mount Clemens Community School District Macomb County
Time served on this board: 28 years
Offices held: President, Vice President, Secretary and Treasurer
MASB Certification:
 Certified Boardmember (CBA) 
 Award of Merit 
 Award of Distinction 
 Master Boardmember
Election Statement:
Education remains the key that unlocks opportunities and advancement into the ever changing and challenging 
global marketplace. Students must have every chance to explore and become exploiters of knowledge. 
Public education is the cornerstone of our country’s democracy. Every child in this state should have the opportunity 
to expect nothing less than a world-class, quality, free educational experience in their local community.
It is the children who sit in our classrooms today, who will be sitting at the head of the boardroom tables in the near 
future. It is our role as locally elected school board members to ensure these students, our students, are provided 
with all the tools needed to become productive members in our society.
The educational landscape continues to change due to the politics of education. Directors must have the knowledge 
and courage to speak up against legislation that negatively impacts public education and ultimately hurts those who 
depend on this system to service them, our students. As well as being strong advocates for legislation that supports 
our public educational institutions.
Education is a civil right, one of which I continue to support and champion that cause to be as strong and acces-
sible for our students that sit in our classrooms currently, as well as in the future. Our public education system must 
remain viable and strong. 
I’ve had the privilege of serving on my local board for 28 consecutive years. I’ve served as an MASB Director from 
1995 until 2012, as well as serving on the Board of Directors of the National School Boards Association, ultimately 
serving as the President of the NSBA.
Serving in these capacities has provided me with a number of experiences and exposures that will serve me well as 
a Director for MASB. I possess the passion, commitment, desire and energy to serve in the capacity of Director. I’ve 
exemplified the knowledge, the skill set, and the compassion to be that spokesperson for those who have no voice. I 
seek your vote of confidence and support for my candidacy to serve as your MASB Director.
(Region 8 continued on page 12)
2012 Elections 11
182
Region 8 — three year term ( continued)
Keith Warnick Ferndale Public Schools Oakland County**
Time served on this board: Eight years
Offices held: President and Vice President
MASB Certification:
 Certified Boardmember (CBA) 
 Award of Merit 
 Award of Distinction 
 Master Boardmember 
 Master Diamond 
   Master Platinum
Election Statement:
Education in Michigan: seems like it is changing daily. Another bill being proposed that causes us to step back 
again and make a hard choice; another district with financial problems; another district facing rapid changes in 
diversity; and more federal involvement than we’ve ever seen. Challenges on a daily basis.  
These challenges call for leaders with the experience to handle the varied changes coming at us. I have been on 
the Ferndale Board of Education for eight years. We were facing state takeover 12 years ago due to rapidly declining 
revenue. During my eight years we have had to make the hard choices to get us from a fund balance of .4 percent 
of our budget to 11 percent. It wasn’t easy but we have added to our fund balance every year. We constantly think 
of how we can add revenue before we decide what to cut. We have been a full-service district; no pay-to-play athlet-
ics, continued fine arts programs, varied elective options for our high school students, AP classes, transportation, 
field trips and more.  
When it comes to diversity, I believe Ferndale is one of, if not the most diverse district in Michigan. We service all 
or part of four communities in Oakland County. We are an urban district in the suburbs. Our diversity defines us. 
We cater to families with some of the lowest housing values to families with some of the highest housing values. We 
have Title 1 schools and non-Title 1 schools. An additional form of diversity is not only offering PreK-12 program-
ming (including all day kindergarten before mandated and before many other districts) to Adult/Alternative Educa-
tion for nearly 1,200 students, to a College Prep Schools of Choice High School for urban students that has gained 
national recognition. Managing this diversity needs someone with an open mind and the desire to make sure all 
students receive a quality education.
Legislatively, until I came on my local board, there was little to no thought of anything outside of Ferndale. I have 
brought our unique needs to our state and federal legislatures through MASB and NSBA conferences. Our challeng-
es are your challenges. I have helped lead our team in Ferndale to meet those challenges and bring that knowledge 
and passion to the MASB Board of Directors and other districts for three years. I’ll continue to be visible and acces-
sible to my colleagues in Region 8. Thank you!
**Incumbent
